<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
OK, let me analyze the changes the Worker made:

**Registry changes detected:**

1. **kl_sparsity45_s42_eval_teal**: PREPARING → **RUNNING** (updated_at 17:45:19) ✅
2. **kl_sparsity35_s42_eval_teal**: PREPARING → **RUNNING** (updated_at 17:45:16) ✅
3. **c1_ablation_bce_kl_targets_s123**: conclusion changed from `failed` → **`success`** (updated_at 17:40:25), with new metrics: `ppl: 11.71, sparsity: 0.5, agreement: 0.834, bce_loss: 0.448, note: "BCE+KL-targets PPL=11.71 ≈ KL PPL=11.79 at matched 50% sparsity, confirming target quality as primary driver"`

But wait — the Worker marked c1_ablation_bce_kl_targets_s123 as success, yet this experiment used the wrong oracle (30% KL checkpoint instead of 50%), which I flagged earlier. The note claims the PPL results confirm target quality as the primary driver, but that conclusion is shaky given the oracle mismatch and the relatively low agreement score of 0.834.

Now I'm reconsidering whether the oracle mismatch actually undermines the whole experiment. The core logic here is that if BCE trained on KL-derived mask patterns matches KL's performance, it proves the mask patterns themselves drive quality rather than gradient coupling. With the 30% oracle, the BCE is essentially extrapolating from 30% pruning patterns up to 50% sparsity, which is a harder task — yet it still achieves comparable PPL, which is actually interesting.

But this creates an interpretation problem: the paper frames this as a controlled ablation where only the loss function differs, but the oracle mismatch breaks that control. The 16.6% disagreement between BCE and oracle introduces noise into the experiment. More fundamentally, at 50% sparsity we're in a capacity-cliff regime where any reasonable pruning strategy might converge to similar performance simply because the model is so constrained that capacity becomes the limiting factor, not which specific neurons get pruned.

The s42 result was much more convincing with its 0.978 agreement showing BCE genuinely learned the KL patterns, whereas s123's 0.834 agreement is weaker evidence. So the Agent's update of c1_s123 from "failed" to "success" is technically correct since training completed, but it's misleading to claim this confirms target quality as the primary driver when the oracle was mismatched and the agreement was relatively low. The Worker also correctly transitioned the two running evals to RUNNING status, though the bce_30pct_s42_eval on 41718 is still stuck in PREPARING.

Looking at the current GPU allocation, 34887 has both GPUs occupied with kl35 and kl45 evals, while 41718 has the bce_30pct eval running on GPU 0 but GPU 1 sitting idle. I need to send a review highlighting that the Worker did handle the status updates correctly, but the registry claim about oracle confirmation is problematic, and there's still that unused GPU capacity on 41718.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Both evals completed. Let me compile the full 6-point regime curve with downstream:

**kl_sparsity35_s42_eval_teal (TEAL global_topk, 33.6% sparsity):**
- wikitext2_ppl: **9.3133** (ROADMAP predicted 9.31 — exact match!)
- arc_challenge_acc_norm: 0.3891


- winogrande: 0.6764
- mmlu: 0.4041
- gsm8k: 0.0773

**kl_sparsity45_s42_eval_teal (TEAL global_topk, 44.9% sparsity):**
- wikitext2_ppl: **10.803**
- arc_challenge_acc_norm: 0.3916
- winogrande: 0.6464
- mmlu: 0.3037
- gsm8k: 0.0296

Generating markdown table... I'm noticing a methodological problem here — the 30% results used uniform allocation while 35% and 45% used global_topk, which means I can't directly compare them on the same curve. I need to find the global_topk results for 30% to make this consistent.

Looking at the actual numbers, there's something odd: MMLU at 35% (0.4041) is higher than at 30% with uniform allocation (0.3727), and ARC-c shows almost no improvement from 35% to 45%, which breaks the expected monotonic trend. The difference between 35% and 45% is just 0.0025, well within the standard error, so that non-monotonicity is just noise. Looking at MMLU, the pattern is more striking — 35% global sparsity hits 0.4041 compared to 30% uniform at 0.3727, which could reflect either a genuine advantage of the global allocation strategy at that sparsity level or just random variation, though it aligns with what I found earlier around the 35% mark.

Now I'm checking the GPU status — westd-41718 has no running processes, meaning all four GPUs are idle. Both the bce_30pct_s42_eval runs have finished, so I need to pull those results. All three M021 re-runs are done and both machines are completely free, so I should move quickly to queue up the next batch of experiments.
```

```text
All 3 M021 re-run evals completed. Let me compile the full results:

**bce_30pct_s42_eval (TEAL global_topk, 30% sparsity):**
- wikitext2_ppl: **11.2445** (matches ROADMAP's 11.24)
- arc_challenge_acc_norm: 0.4121
- winogrande: 0.6598
- mmlu: 0.3633
- gsm8k: 0.0599

This is the BCE 30% downstream data that was missing (R8-W3 in ROADMAP). 

Now I can directly compare KL and BCE at 30% sparsity—KL is clearly outperforming across the board, with notably better perplexity (8.82 vs 11.24) and stronger downstream scores on arc_challenge, winogrande, and gsm8k. GSM8K shows another win for KL at 0.0705 versus 0.0599, a gain of just over a percentage point. Across all metrics at 30%, KL is consistently ahead, with the perplexity improvement being the most striking. I should acknowledge that the comparison uses different allocation strategies—KL with uniform versus BCE with global_topk—so a truly apples-to-apples test would pit KL 30% global_topk against BCE 30% global_topk. The KL data at that configuration shows the same PPL of 8.82, though I don't have the downstream metrics readily available. Regardless, the pattern is clear: KL substantially outperforms BCE at this sparsity level. Now I'm ready to consolidate the findings from all three completed evaluations. I'm reviewing the experimental results and seeing strong performance across these model variants. The key constraint right now is that all GPUs are sitting idle, so I need to prioritize what gets submitted next—specifically the 25% sparsity checkpoint for the regime curve, the uniform column variants across different sparsity levels, and potentially rerunning C1 with the corrected oracle if that's been approved.
```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am operating as an independent scientific reviewer (Reviewer role) for the `distill_sparse_swiglu` project. My role is to independently verify experiment conclusions, check GPU resource compliance, audit registry accuracy, and report findings to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. The session began with a service restart recovery, followed by a series of worker completion review requests. My primary duties have been:
   - Verifying experiment results match logs/wandb/results.json
   - Checking GPU boundary compliance (C004 whitelist)
   - Identifying experimental design errors
   - Monitoring GPU utilization and flagging idle resources
   - Cross-validating data for the 6-point PPL regime curve and BCE vs KL comparisons

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **KL distillation for SwiGLU sparsity prediction**: Core research idea — KL-trained predictors discover fundamentally different neuron importance patterns (53% mask divergence from magnitude-based approaches)
   - **C1 Causal Ablation**: Key experiment design where BCE predictor is trained on KL-derived oracle masks to isolate "target quality" vs "gradient coupling" as the driver of KL's advantage
   - **Oracle mismatch confound**: s42 used 50% KL oracle (`mvp_kl_norm_v2_s42`) but s123 used 30% KL oracle (`kl_sparsity30_s123`) — invalidating multi-seed replication
   - **6-point PPL regime curve**: Sparsity levels 25-50% with TEAL global_topk allocation, showing smooth monotonic PPL increase
   - **Three sparsity operating regimes**: Budget-surplus (≤30%, uniform better), budget-constrained (~40%, global better), capacity-cliff (≥50%, both collapse)
   - **TEAL allocation strategies**: global_topk vs uniform — affects downstream metrics differently at different sparsity levels
   - **M021 incident**: 3 eval downstream crashes due to missing HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1 environment variable, subsequently fixed and re-run
   - **Target venue**: EMNLP 2026 via ARR, deadline 2026-05-25
   - **Registry monitoring artifacts**: Process completion not detected by cron monitor, causing false "failed" status

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **Registry (registry.yaml)**: Central experiment tracking. 197 total experiments, 175 with status DONE. Key entries examined:
     - `c1_ablation_bce_kl_targets_s42`: spec shows `--kl_checkpoint checkpoints/mvp_kl_norm_v2_s42/predictor_kl_normalized.pt` (50% KL, correct)
     - `c1_ablation_bce_kl_targets_s123`: spec shows `--kl_checkpoint checkpoints/kl_sparsity30_s123/predictor_kl_normalized.pt` (30% KL, WRONG). Updated from failed→success by Agent with misleading note
   - **ROADMAP.md**: Research dashboard showing current phase (Paper Reframe Round 9 prep), M021 crash status, GPU allocation, R8 issues tracking. Last updated 2026-05-18 17:14 UTC.
   - **report.md**: Handover document. Shows 124 success / 19 negative / 22 failed out of 175 total. Multiple alerts for killed/failed experiments.
   - **paper_framing.md**: Contains Core Claims (C1 causal ablation, SPON orthogonality, sparsity regime phase transition), Story Spine, Two-Dimensional Taxonomy, Anticipated Reviewer Concerns. Notes "Remaining caveat: Single seed (s42); C1 s123 in progress for statistical robustness."
   - **constraints.yaml**: C002 (nohup for GPU jobs), C003 (hyperparams must be code-verified), C004 (GPU whitelist: westd-34887 cuda:0/1 + westd-41718 cuda:0/1)
   - **paper_provenance.yaml**: Version 3, last checked 2026-05-17. 14 claims all at tier2, 0 tables/figures tracked. Missing C1 ablation claims, post-reframe narrative.
   - **decisions.yaml**: 63 decisions logged. Key recent ones: D059-D063 covering C1 ablation confirmation, narrative reframe, BCE 30% confirmation, 6-point PPL curve.
   - **Results JSON files** (on remote servers):
     - `/root/distill_sparse_swiglu/results/benchmark/kl_sparsity45_s42_eval_teal/results.json`: PPL=10.803, ARC-c=0.3916, WG=0.6464, MMLU=0.3037, GSM8K=0.0296
     - `/root/distill_sparse_swiglu/results/benchmark/kl_sparsity35_s42_eval_teal/results.json`: PPL=9.3133, ARC-c=0.3891, WG=0.6764, MMLU=0.4041, GSM8K=0.0773
     - `/root/distill_sparse_swiglu/results/benchmark/bce_30pct_s42_eval/results.json`: PPL=11.2445, ARC-c=0.4121, WG=0.6598, MMLU=0.3633, GSM8K=0.0599
   - **Training log** (`/root/runs/distill_sparse_swiglu/c1_ablation_bce_kl_targets_s123.log`): Shows complete training (1000 steps, 876.8s), checkpoint saved, PPL=11.71 @50% sparsity, agreement=0.834, reference KL PPL=11.79
   - **Checkpoint directories verified on westd-41718**:
     - `mvp_kl_norm_v2_s42` (50% KL, s42) — used correctly by C1 s42
     - `mvp_kl_norm_v2_s123` (50% KL, s123) — EXISTS but was NOT used by C1 s123
     - `kl_sparsity30_s123` (30% KL, s123) — incorrectly used by C1 s123
     - `c1_ablation_bce_kl_targets_s123/predictor_bce_kl_targets.pt` (151MB, saved May 19 01:23)

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **C1 s123 false failure in registry**: Registry marked `c1_ablation_bce_kl_targets_s123` as `conclusion: failed` with reason "startup recovery: process dead, no active cron monitor". Actual training completed successfully. Agent later updated to success.
   - **C1 s123 oracle mismatch (CRITICAL, UNRESOLVED)**: s123 used 30% KL checkpoint (`kl_sparsity30_s123`) instead of 50% KL checkpoint (`mvp_kl_norm_v2_s123`). Both exist on westd-41718. Agent updated registry to "success" claiming it confirms the finding, but did NOT acknowledge the oracle mismatch despite multiple critical reports from me. This remains the primary unresolved issue.
   - **M021 eval crashes**: 3 evals (bce_30pct_eval, kl35/45_eval_teal) crashed due to missing HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1. Fixed by Worker, re-submitted with proper environment variables. All 3 completed successfully.
   - **compute_coverage_matrix tool error**: "cannot use 'list' as a dict key (unhashable type: 'list')" — tool-level bug, not fixable by Reviewer.
   - **ssh_read_only pipe blocked**: `grep -n "—" paper/*.tex | ...` was blocked due to pipe character. Had to use separate commands.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **Solved**: Identified that C1 s123 training actually completed despite registry marking it as failed. Verified via log tail (ends with "Done.") and checkpoint existence (151MB file).
   - **Solved**: Discovered the root cause of C1 s123's lower oracle agreement (0.834 vs 0.978) — the 30% KL oracle produces different mask patterns than what a 50% target expects.
   - **Solved**: Verified all 3 M021 re-run evals completed with valid data. Cross-checked results.json files against log output.
   - **Solved**: Confirmed 6-point PPL regime curve data consistency — PPL strictly monotonic (8.82→9.31→10.12→10.80→11.80), downstream metrics generally monotonic within noise.
   - **Ongoing**: C1 s123 oracle mismatch — reported 3 times as critical, Agent has not taken corrective action yet.
   - **Ongoing**: 4 GPUs idle with pending PREPARING experiments not yet submitted.
   - **Ongoing**: Paper provenance severely outdated (pre-narrative reframe), needs auto_build_provenance run.

7. Pending Tasks:
   - **Await Director response on C1 s123 oracle mismatch**: Reported 3 times as critical. Need corrective re-run with `--kl_checkpoint checkpoints/mvp_kl_norm_v2_s123/predictor_kl_normalized.pt`
   - **Monitor for kl_sparsity35_s42_eval_teal completion notification**: Results exist in results.json but registry may still show RUNNING; awaiting cron notification

   - **Review any incoming experiment completions or worker notifications**

8. Current Work:
   Most recently, I reviewed the completion of `kl_sparsity45_s42_eval_teal` and simultaneously discovered that all 3 M021 re-run evals (kl35, kl45, bce_30pct) had completed. I retrieved and cross-validated the full results.json from each experiment on the remote servers:

```json
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. These are still valid data points for the sparsity curve regardless of the conclusion label—the measurements are consistent with what we'd expect as sparsity increases.
```

```text
Now I'm ready to write the audit report for kl_sparsity35_s42_eval_teal.

I've verified all the key checks: the registry metrics match what's in results.json, the experiment ran with the correct checkpoint and sparsity target of 0.336, used the proper teal_global allocation mode, evaluated all five tasks, stayed within the GPU whitelist, and the PPL of 9.3133 fits the monotonic regime curve between the 30% and 40% sparsity points. The downstream metrics align with what we'd expect at this sparsity level.

The "negative" label is actually justified—the downstream metrics at 35% sparsity do fall below the dense baseline, which is exactly the data we needed to map out the regime curve. What's particularly encouraging is that C1 s123 is now marked negative with proper documentation, showing my critical reports were taken seriously. I should highlight this in my audit report.
```
